🖥️Menu UI Modes

🎨UI Modes

Menus can be presented as Inventory slots, native Form/Dialog controls, or graphical cards. A menu's type determines which presentations it supports; switching presentations changes only the UI, never product prices, conditions, or saved data.

Available Presentations

Presentation

Client & Requirements

Layout Setup

Inventory

Java & Bedrock, standard chest screen

Root size, layout, buttons

Bedrock Form - Premium

Bedrock with supported Geyser / Floodgate

bedrock.*; entries become a list

Native Dialog - Premium

Java on supported Paper 1.21.9+

dialog.layout: multi-action or item-action-list

Ore - Premium

Supported Paper Dialog runtime + generated resource pack

dialog.layout: ore + graphical ui

ArcMenu - Premium

Java with compatible ArcMenu plugin & client setup

arcmenu.enabled + graphical ui

Native multi-action and item-action-list do not require an Ore resource pack. ArcMenu client requirements follow its own integration.

Normal Selection Order

Global switches in config.yml:

Copy

menu:
dialog:
enabled: true
bedrock:
enabled: true
arcmenu:
enabled: false

Individual menu files can restrict further:

Java players: tried in order ArcMenu → Dialog / Ore → Inventory. If ArcMenu is unavailable, it falls back to enabled and supported Dialog or Inventory; dialog.layout chooses native layout vs. Ore.

Bedrock players: checked separately for Bedrock Form. To keep both clients on Inventory, use force-ui below.

By default, a menu's dialog.enabled and bedrock.enabled are true, while arcmenu.enabled is false. Enabling a global switch alone does not guarantee runtime or menu-type compatibility.

Supported Menu Types

Menu Type

Inventory

Bedrock Form

Native Dialog

Ore

ArcMenu

Common / Shop

Yes

Yes

Yes

Yes

Yes

Search / Favourite

Yes

Yes

Yes

Falls back to native multi-action

Yes

Buy More

Slot controls

Source shop quantity form

Native quantity input

No separate graphical UI

Source screen product details

Item Sell / Sell All

Yes

No

No

No

No

In-Game Editor

Yes

No

No

No

No

Notes:

Search and Favourite menus with dialog.layout: ore fall back to native multi-action and generate no Ore assets.

Buy More's Inventory layout is not converted into Form or graphical canvases.

Item Sell and Sell All always open a physical Inventory screen, even when triggered from another presentation.

Force One Presentation

Force a presentation globally in config.yml:

This overrides global and per-menu switches while still respecting platform, version, integration, and menu-type requirements:

Forced type

Java

Bedrock

inventory

Inventory

Inventory

arcmenu

ArcMenu when available, else Inventory

Inventory

dialog

Native Dialog when available (Ore also becomes multi-action)

Inventory

item-action-list

Native item-action-list when supported

Inventory

ore

Ore for Common/Shop; native multi-action for Search/Favourite

Inventory

form

Inventory

Bedrock Form when available

Unsupported types still fall back (for example, Item Sell and Sell All stay Inventory; forcing dialog leaves existing item-action-list menus as item-action-list).

Set style to a style ID like bedrock to force graphical defaults too, or leave '' to keep per-menu style selection; see Menu Configuration & Inheritance. Invalid type values log a config error and fall back to normal selection.

Configure Layout, Content, and Visibility

What to Change

Where

Inventory slot positions

Root size, layout

Native Dialog controls

dialog.*

Bedrock Form text & behaviour

bedrock.*

Graphical style

Global menu.ui.style or menu ui.style

Ore / ArcMenu coordinates

ui.layout and entry ui.placement

Product card defaults

ui.products.*

Hide an entry in one presentation

Entry menu-visibility.<presentation>

Root content is the shared fallback description, overridden by dialog.content, bedrock.content, or arcmenu.content. Graphical ui controls drawing and layout only—there is no ui.mode selector.

Text Width in ArcMenu and Dialog

UltimateShop calculates text width using plugins/UltimateShop/glyph-widths.yml. If your server uses non-English characters, default widths may cause truncation, unexpected wrapping, or misalignment in ArcMenu or Dialog (including Ore).

Configure pixel widths in glyph-widths.yml under shared.width for single code points (e.g. U+0041) or inclusive ranges (e.g. U+0041-U+005A); later rules override earlier ones, and ore.width overrides shared.width for Ore. Run /shop reload and reopen the menu to verify.

Menu System · Layout & Buttons · Graphical UI Layout

🔌Menus — Getting Started

🧱Layout & Buttons

Root layout maps entry IDs to Inventory slots: common buttons are defined under buttons, while shop menus can also reference shop products and shop buttons. Ore and ArcMenu use separate graphical placement settings.

Complete Button Menu Example

Save this as menus/common_menus/categories.yml:

Copy

title: '&8Categories'
size: 18
layout:
- '000000000'
- '000A0X000'
buttons:
A:
display-item:
material: WHEAT
name: '&aFarming'
actions:
1:
type: shop_menu
shop: farming
X:
display-item:
material: BARRIER
name: '&cClose'
actions:
1:
type: close

Create the farming shop, run /shop reload, and open /shop menu categories. In Inventory UI, slots are zero-indexed, placing A in slot 12 and X in slot 14.

Slot IDs

layout is read left-to-right, top-to-bottom. Each row has 9 slots, and size must match the total slot count (9, 18, 27, 36, 45, or 54).

Case-sensitive: A and a are distinct IDs.

Repeatable: repeating an ID places the same entry in multiple slots.

Empty slots: undefined IDs stay empty.

0 is a normal ID: defining buttons.'0' fills every 0 slot (commonly used for filler glass panes).

Quote numeric YAML keys such as '0'. The snippet above also shows how to keep filler panes in Inventory while hiding them in Form, Dialog, and graphical UIs.

Multi-Character IDs

Wrap multi-character IDs in backticks (`) inside layout. Each backtick token occupies one slot:

This row defines 9 slots: 0, food_buy, food_sell, and six 0 slots. Backticks are layout delimiters only—do not include them in buttons keys.

Shop Menu Entry Sources

A shop menu template's layout can reference three sources:

Source

Location

Products

Shop items.<id>

Shop-specific common buttons

Shop buttons.<id>

Reusable common buttons

Template buttons.<id>

Use distinct IDs across sources. If an ID collides, the plugin picks the first eligible match in this priority order: copied shop entries → shop buttons → shop items → template buttons.

Common buttons run their own actions, whereas products use shop trade logic and click bindings (buy, sell, select-amount, etc.). A navigation button never becomes a tradable product just by sharing a material.

Button Actions & Conditions

Key

Purpose

display-item

Button appearance; see Display Item Format

actions

Actions run when click conditions pass

conditions

Conditions checked on click

fail-actions

Actions run when click conditions fail

display-conditions

Controls whether the button is shown (Premium)

menu-visibility

Per-presentation visibility

dialog.layout, dialog.show-sprite

Native Dialog appearance for this entry

ui

Ore / ArcMenu appearance and placement

The example below keeps the button visible but checks permission on click:

conditions blocks clicks without hiding the button; use display-conditions to hide entries. Root menu conditions control opening the whole screen, while product buy/sell conditions validate trades separately.

Per-Presentation Visibility

Add menu-visibility at a button or product root:

Unspecified keys default to true. If ore or item-action-list is omitted, it falls back to menu-visibility.dialog (so dialog: false hides all three unless overridden).

menu-visibility only filters existing entries—it never creates slots, graphical placements, or click bindings, and works independently of dynamic-layout.

Per-Player Dynamic Layout - Premium

When dynamic-layout: true is enabled, layout placeholders and display-conditions are evaluated per player. Use || inside a backtick token to list fallback candidates in priority order:

This row still has 9 slots. At runtime, the plugin checks A then B, picking the first entry that exists, is visible in the current presentation, and passes display-conditions (or leaves the slot empty if none pass). Put your fallback entry last; see Conditional Display.

Entry Placement Across Presentations

Presentation

Placement Source

Inventory

Root layout slots

Bedrock Form

Ordered list of entries (empty Inventory spacers ignored)

Native Dialog

Action/list entries read in root layout order

Ore / ArcMenu

Named graphical areas and ui.placement

Common graphical buttons must set ui.placement explicitly (and will appear in graphical UIs even if omitted from root layout):

Products inherit ui.products.placement and merge their own ui.placement overrides. Empty areas never auto-generate Back, Close, or category buttons.

Entry Placement Across Presentations

layout:
- '000000000'
buttons:
'0':
display-item:
material: GRAY_STAINED_GLASS_PANE
name: ' '
menu-visibility:
form: false
dialog: false
ore: false
arcmenu: false

Copy

layout:
- '0`food_buy``food_sell`000000'
buttons:
food_buy:
display-item:
material: EMERALD
name: '&aBuy food'
food_sell:
display-item:
material: GOLD_INGOT
name: '&eSell food'

Copy

buttons:
A:
display-item:
material: DIAMOND
name: '&bVIP Shop'
conditions:
1:
type: permission
permission: shop.vip
actions:
1:
type: shop_menu
shop: vip
fail-actions:
1:
type: message
message: '&cYou do not have permission!'

Copy

menu-visibility:
inventory: true
form: true
dialog: true
item-action-list: false
ore: false
arcmenu: true

Copy

dynamic-layout: true
layout:
- '000`A||B`00000'

Copy

buttons:
A:
display-item:

🔌Menus — Getting Started

⚙️Menu Configuration & Inheritance

Menu configuration is split across three layers: config.yml holds global defaults, ui/ stores reusable graphical styles, and menus/ defines individual screens. Shop files select a menu template and can override template settings per shop.

File Locations

Copy

plugins/UltimateShop/
├── config.yml
├── ui/
│ ├── classic.yml
│ └── bedrock.yml
├── menus/
│ ├── common_menus/
│ ├── shop_menu_templates/
│ ├── buy_more_menus/
│ ├── search_menus/
│ ├── favourite_menus/
│ └── item_sell_menus/
└── shops/

Menu and shop IDs are file names without .yml, even if placed inside subfolders. Graphical style IDs, however, include their relative subfolder path (for example, ui/custom/dark.yml has ID custom/dark).

Global vs. Menu Settings

Configure global defaults in config.yml:

In individual menu files, place the corresponding keys at the root (without the outer menu: wrapper):

During normal selection, both the global switch and the menu's own switch must allow a presentation for it to be used. By default, a menu's dialog.enabled and bedrock.enabled are true, while arcmenu.enabled is false. Enabling global menu.force-ui overrides these switches (though platform and menu-type limits still apply); see UI Modes.

Native Dialog and Form labels/options read the menu's dialog.* / bedrock.* first, falling back to global menu.dialog.* / menu.bedrock.* (some integration and pack-generation options are global-only). Screen descriptions read dialog.content, bedrock.content, or arcmenu.content, falling back to root content.

Shop Templates and Overrides

A shop selects a template from menus/shop_menu_templates/ (such as farming.yml) and can override specific fields under settings.menu-settings:

settings.menu-settings lives in the shop file. It merges onto the chosen template before graphical defaults are applied:

Override Type

Merge Behaviour

Scalars (title, enabled, etc.)

Replaces the template value

Nested sections (Maps)

Deep-merges recursively with the template

Lists (layout, lore, etc.)

Replaces the entire list

Omitted keys

Keeps the template value

For example, overriding dialog.layout leaves dialog.columns from the template intact, whereas overriding layout requires supplying all rows. Shops without settings.menu can define their entire screen directly inside settings.menu-settings.

Shared Graphical Styles

ui/classic.yml and ui/bedrock.yml are the built-in graphical presets (the plugin only creates missing files and never overwrites your edits). Copy a preset to ui/my-style.yml to customize it.

Style files contain UI fields directly at the root, without an outer ui: key:

If a style file only defines a few fields, referencing ui.style: my-style inherits the remaining fields from the global graphical defaults. For a fully independent layout, copy an entire built-in preset first.

Set the global style in config.yml, or pick a different style per menu:

Normal graphical inheritance order:

Load the global style file, then apply explicit menu.ui overrides from config.yml.

If the menu selects a different ui.style, merge that style on top of the resolved global defaults.

Fill any missing ui fields on the menu; explicit fields on the menu (including shop menu-settings) always win.

Graphical styles only affect Ore and ArcMenu layouts and visuals. ui.style: bedrock is a visual preset and never turns a Java screen into a Bedrock Form.

Force a Presentation and Style

Configure menu.force-ui in config.yml:

type forces the presentation globally across supported menus.

When style is non-empty, its resolved defaults overwrite the corresponding graphical fields on menus (local fields not defined in the forced style are kept).

Leave style: '' to force only the presentation while keeping per-menu style selection; enabled: false disables both. See UI Modes.

Where Product & Button Settings Live

Purpose

Location

Common button item & actions

Menu buttons.<id>.display-item / actions

Product item, prices & trade rules

Shop items.<id>

Per-presentation visibility

Button or product root menu-visibility.<presentation>

Graphical product placement

Menu ui.products.placement (overridden by product ui.placement)

Common graphical button placement

Button ui.placement

Graphical product price & actions

Product ui.price / ui.actions → owning shop menu ui.products.* → global menu.ui.products.*

Graphical card styling

Menu ui.products.card

Bedrock Form icon & extra line

Active display-item.bedrock.*

Bedrock Form direct product action

Product bedrock.click-event

Products shown in Search or Favourite menus keep their source shop's trade rules and settings; menu-level graphical defaults for actions and prices come from the product's owning shop menu.

Menu-Type Scope

Different menu types support different levels of graphical styling:

Common & Shop menus: full support for both Ore and ArcMenu defaults and card settings.

Search & Favourite menus: support ArcMenu styling, but do not use Ore (they ignore Ore templates and card description defaults).

Item Sell & Buy More menus: do not inherit global graphical menu.ui settings. Buy More only checks its own dialog.enabled to decide whether to use native quantity input, while Form/Dialog labels come from the source shop.

Sell All menu: a standalone global chest screen that reads only config.yml → menu.sell-all.

Run /shop reload after editing menus, styles, or selectors. If you edit Ore layouts or textures, also rebuild and distribute the resource pack.

Layout & Buttons · Graphical UI Layout

🔌Menus — Getting Started

📚Menu System

Menus define the screens players open in-game. A menu's type determines its content and interaction flow, while its presentation determines whether players see an Inventory, Bedrock Form, native Java Dialog, Ore, or ArcMenu screen. Product prices, stock limits, and trade conditions always remain in the shop configuration.

All file paths in this section are relative to plugins/UltimateShop/. Menu and shop IDs are file names without the .yml extension—keep IDs unique within their folders so commands and actions open the intended screen.

Create Your First Menu

Save this complete example as menus/common_menus/hello.yml:

Copy

title: '&8Hello'
size: 9
layout:
- '0000X0000'
dialog:
enabled: false
bedrock:
enabled: false
arcmenu:
enabled: false
buttons:
X:
display-item:
material: BARRIER
name: '&cClose'
actions:
1:
type: close

Run /shop reload, then /shop menu hello. This opens a one-row Inventory menu with a Close button in the fifth slot. Other presentations are disabled here; if menu.force-ui is enabled globally in config.yml, the forced UI takes priority.

In layout, each character represents a slot: X maps to buttons.X, while undefined 0 entries stay empty. See Layout & Buttons for multi-character IDs, click actions, conditions, and visibility controls.

Choose a Menu Type

Type

Configuration Path

Player Flow

Common

menus/common_menus/<id>.yml

Category navigation, info panels, and custom actions

Shop

shops/<id>.yml and menus/shop_menu_templates/<template>.yml

Browse shop products and buy/sell

Buy More

menus/buy_more_menus/<id>.yml

Choose a trade quantity for a selected product

Search - Premium

menus/search_menus/<id>.yml

Search visible shop products by item or name

Favourite - Premium

menus/favourite_menus/<id>.yml

Save favourite products and reorder them

Item Sell

menus/item_sell_menus/<id>.yml

Deposit items, preview payout, and confirm

Sell All

config.yml → menu.sell-all

Deposit items and sell automatically on close

Use /shop menu <id> to open Common, Shop, Search, Favourite, and Item Sell menus. Buy More requires a selected product first; Sell All opens via /shop sellall or the sell_all_menu action.

Choose a Presentation

Inventory screens use the root size and layout. Premium adds Bedrock Form, native Java Dialog, graphical Ore, and ArcMenu, depending on the menu type, server platform, and installed integrations (see UI Modes).

Graphical styles live in ui/. Select a shared style via global menu.ui.style or a menu's ui.style, and control coordinates with ui.layout and entry ui.placement.

ui.style: bedrock is only a graphical appearance preset for Ore and ArcMenu—it is completely separate from Bedrock Form UI.

Next Steps

Menu Configuration & Inheritance: file structure, shop templates, styles, and override priority.

Layout & Buttons: slot IDs, actions, conditions, and per-presentation visibility.

Graphical UI Layout - Premium: canvas, areas, product cards, pagination, and themes.

Conditional Display - Premium: per-player entries and fallback slots.

Display Item Add Lore: prices, limits, and presentation-specific hints.

Menu Configuration Compatibility: current keys and migration notes.

Shared formats: Display Item Format, Action Format, and Condition Format.

🔌Menus — Getting Started

📐Graphical UI Layout - Premium

Ore and ArcMenu share the same graphical styles, logical canvas, named areas, and entry placement system. These settings only affect graphical renderers; Inventory uses root layout, while Bedrock Form and native Dialog use their own controls.

Common and Shop menus support both Ore and ArcMenu, Search and Favourite support ArcMenu, and Buy More / Item Sell do not use this canvas; see UI Modes.

Start from a Shared Style

Select a global menu.ui.style in config.yml, or ui.style at a menu's root. Built-in classic uses a left sidebar and bedrock uses a top bar; both provide sidebar, content, and footer areas. Style files live in plugins/UltimateShop/ui/ and contain UI fields directly at the root (no outer ui: key).

Complete Common menu example using an inherited canvas:

Copy

title: '&8Categories'
size: 9
layout:
- '0000A0000'
dialog:
enabled: true
layout: ore
arcmenu:
enabled: false
ui:
style: classic
buttons:
A:
display-item:
material: WHEAT
name: '&aFarming'
actions:
1:
type: shop_menu
shop: farming
ui:
appearance: button
placement:
area: sidebar
order: 0

Make sure the target shop exists. For Ore, enable global dialog, reload, and generate/distribute the resource pack. For ArcMenu, install a compatible integration and enable global/menu switches. Note that root Inventory layout does not determine graphical coordinates.

Shop product cards usually inherit a shared area placement:

Configuration Scope

Scope

Configuration

Shared style file

ui/<style>.yml: layout, products, theme, text, renderers

Global graphical defaults

config.yml → menu.ui

Menu overrides

Menu root ui; shops can also use settings.menu-settings.ui

Per-product actions/price

Product root ui.actions, ui.price

Per-entry placement

Product or button root ui.placement

Common entry appearance

Entry ui.appearance, ui.font-size, ui.text-align

Under normal selection, explicit menu fields override style defaults, and switching styles overrides global defaults before filling missing fields recursively. Forced styles can override local defaults; see Menu Configuration & Inheritance.

Graphical product actions/prices resolve in order: product → owning shop menu → global defaults. Card styling applies menu-wide. Bedrock Form price lines use bedrock.price-extra-line, independent of graphical prices.

Canvas and Areas

Full classic-style canvas snippet:

Because sections merge recursively, editing header.title does not remove other inherited headers; set enabled: false to hide unwanted headers.

Path relative to ui.layout

Purpose

canvas.width, canvas.height

Logical dimensions: width 144–900, height 144–540

header.<id>.enabled / text

Visibility and text of a named header

header.<id>.x / y / width / height

Header bounds (default height 27)

header.<id>.align

left, center, or right

header.<id>.font-size, bold

ArcMenu text scale and bold

areas.<id>.type

grid, stack, or free

areas.<id>.x / y / width / height

Area bounds within the canvas

areas.<id>.columns / rows

Grid columns 1–36, rows 1–60

areas.<id>.cell-height

Grid cell height (width is derived from area width, columns, and gap)

areas.<id>.direction

Stack direction: vertical (default) or horizontal

areas.<id>.item-width / item-height

Stack item size (defaults 108 / 36)

areas.<id>.gap, gap-x / gap-y

Item spacing (gap is the fallback for both axes)

areas.<id>.background-enabled / background-padding

ArcMenu area background and padding

pagination.enabled

Enables graphical pagination controls

pagination.previous / indicator / next

Each supports enabled, x, y, width, height

Headers support {title}, {content}, language strings, and PlaceholderAPI. {content} reads dialog.content in Ore, arcmenu.content in ArcMenu, and falls back to root content.

Area Behaviour and Pagination

Grid: places entries with explicit row/column first, then fills remaining cells sequentially. A 3 × 2 grid holds 6 entries per page and paginates overflow; row/column indices are zero-based (rows beyond page 1 spill onto later pages; columns must stay within columns).

Stack: arranges entries by placement.order vertically or horizontally without pagination—all entries must fit. In the built-in bedrock preset, the horizontal sidebar holds 5 navigation buttons and stays visible across grid pages.

Free: positions entries using offsets relative to the area origin; requires both placement.x and placement.y (default size 108 × 27).

Geometry rules: canvas height, all Y coordinates/heights, and vertical gaps must be multiples of 9; bounds cannot exceed their parent area; minimum box size is 24 × 9; non-free cells require height ≥ 27; appearance: card requires height ≥ 99. Visible entries must not overlap each other, headers, or enabled pagination controls.

Search and Favourite menus cap source entries by their root result-slot count before graphical layout runs; enlarging the grid or adding graphical pages does not increase source capacity.

Entry Placement

Here search must exist (Premium) and close is positioned relative to footer. Both appear in graphical UIs even if omitted from root layout.

Entry Key

Purpose

ui.placement.enabled

Defaults to true when placement is present; set false to disable

ui.placement.area

Target named area ID

ui.placement.order

Sort priority (defaults to source slot order)

ui.placement.row / column

Explicit grid cell (setting row enables explicit placement)

ui.placement.x / y

Free-area internal offset

ui.placement.width / height

Overrides entry size

ui.appearance

button or card (products default to card)

ui.font-size

ArcMenu common-button text scale

ui.text-align

Common graphical button text alignment

menu-visibility.ore / arcmenu

Per-entry graphical visibility

Common entries need their own placement; products inherit ui.products.placement and merge their own ui.placement. appearance: card also works on category/navigation buttons.

Product Cards, Price, and Actions

Path relative to ui.products

Purpose

card.icon-size, padding

Item icon size and card padding

card.name-size / price-size

ArcMenu product name and price text scale

card.button-font-size / button-height

ArcMenu action button text scale and height

card.buttons.inset-x / inset-bottom / gap

ArcMenu action button insets and gap

card.lore-lines

Maximum body lore lines on Ore cards

card.description-lines

Maximum Ore category description lines (ArcMenu adapts to space)

price.enabled

Enables the compact price line

price.formats.both / only-buy / only-sell

Price format chosen by available trade directions

actions.primary

Priority-ordered IDs (up to 2 available primary actions shown)

actions.extra

auto for remaining actions, a specific list, or [] for none

actions.labels.<id>

Button label (supports {item-name} and standard placeholders)

Action IDs come from product click bindings and custom menu.click-event-actions (listing buy does not create a missing binding, and actions are filtered by prices, product options, and buy-only/sell-only rules).

Products can override ui.price and ui.actions at their root, while card styling applies menu-wide. The compact price represents one trade unit (with shared dynamic pricing and multipliers); full lore and auto-lore still appear in details/tooltips and can be filtered with presentation conditions.

Theme and Text

Quote hex colors in YAML. ArcMenu supports #RRGGBB and #AARRGGBB, while Ore's generated RGB textures ignore alpha.

Path relative to ui.theme

Purpose

background-enabled, background

ArcMenu window background

sidebar, card, border, accent, text

Shared panel/card/border/accent colors and ArcMenu base text color

button.background / hover / border / text-align

Common graphical button styling

buy.background / hover, sell.background / hover

Trade button styling

title.background-enabled / background / decoration-enabled

Standalone ArcMenu title background and decorations

decorations.enabled / style / color / corner-color

ArcMenu frame and corner accents

decorations.thickness / corner-thickness / corner-length / inset

Decoration sizing

decorations.dividers-enabled

ArcMenu header dividers

bevel.enabled / shadow / top / left / right / bottom

ArcMenu control bevel defaults

tooltip.border / background

ArcMenu hover tooltip colors

details.background, more-menu.background

ArcMenu details panel and extra-actions popup background

ArcMenu's buy, sell, quantity, pagination, and cancel sections also accept per-control text and bevel colors (blended with the control's base color).

Title backgrounds can be enabled independently of the window background:

decorations.style accepts frame, corners, both, or none (none omits the outer frame while still allowing header dividers). Ranges: thickness 0.5–6, corner thickness 0.5–8, corner length 0–80, inset 0–30.

Graphical UI labels live under ui.text (cancel, no-extra, amount-input, select-product, favourite-edit.forward/backward/remove) and support language references.

Renderer-Specific Settings

Ore texture templates live under ui.renderers.ore.templates, while ArcMenu interaction, animation, and tooltip settings live under ui.renderers.arcmenu (update-interval is global-only). Sharing geometry does not guarantee identical font sizing or card body wrapping between the two renderers.

Ore UI · ArcMenu UI · Compatibility

🧭Menu Types

🔲Common Menus

Common menus display custom buttons for main hubs, category navigation, or info screens. Save them in menus/common_menus/ and open them with /shop menu <id> or the open_menu action.

Create a Navigation Menu

Save this as menus/common_menus/main.yml:

Copy

title: '&8Main Menu'
size: 9
layout:
- '000A0X000'
dialog:
enabled: false
bedrock:
enabled: false
arcmenu:
enabled: false
buttons:
A:
display-item:
material: WHEAT
name: '&aFarming'
actions:
1:
type: shop_menu
shop: farming
X:
display-item:
material: BARRIER
name: '&cClose'
actions:
1:
type: close

Make sure the farming shop already exists (otherwise clicking the button will have no shop to open). Save the file, run /shop reload, and then run /shop menu main to open it.

Because dialog, bedrock, and arcmenu are all set to false in this example, it opens as a standard chest Inventory screen (unless global menu.force-ui is enabled in config.yml).

Opening a shop vs. opening another menu:

To open a shop (for buying/selling products), use type: shop_menu with shop: <shop-id>.

To open another Common, Search, Favourite, or Item Sell menu, use type: open_menu with menu: <menu-id>.

Common menus only show the buttons you define under buttons—they never pull in shop products automatically.

Common Menu Keys

Key

Purpose

title

Screen title

content

Screen description (fallback for presentation-specific content)

size, layout

Inventory size and slot layout

buttons

Button display items, actions, and conditions

conditions

Conditions checked before opening the menu

open-actions, close-actions

Actions run on menu open and close

dynamic-layout

Per-player layout resolution (Premium)

dialog, bedrock, arcmenu

Presentation switches and native options

ui

Ore / ArcMenu style, layout, and rendering settings

Keep the three condition scopes distinct: buttons.<id>.conditions checks clicks, buttons.<id>.display-conditions controls visibility, and root conditions controls opening the screen. See Layout & Buttons.

Use Other Presentations - Premium

To use native Dialog instead, replace the dialog block in the example with:

Also enable global menu.dialog.enabled in config.yml. For Ore, set dialog.layout: ore and configure graphical placement and the resource pack; for ArcMenu, install a compatible integration and enable global/menu arcmenu.enabled; for Bedrock Form, enable global/menu bedrock.enabled.

See UI Modes for selection order, forced UIs, and platform support, and Graphical UI Layout for areas and coordinates.

Custom Commands - Premium

Add custom-command at the menu root to register a command for that menu (avoid colliding with existing commands):

Inventory Refresh & Title Updates

In config.yml, menu.menu-update.circle-update refreshes item displays every second and click-update refreshes after clicks. Premium also supports dynamic title updates (menu.title-update.enabled / circle-update / click-update, requiring PacketEvents). See Inventory UI.

Declaring Special Menu Types in common_menus

Although Search, Favourite, and Item Sell menus should live in their dedicated folders (auto-detected by folder), legacy files in menus/common_menus/ can declare menu-type: search, favourite (alias favorite), or item-sell (alias item_sell) at the root (or be listed in the legacy global Search/Favourite lists).

Regardless of folder, available controls and UI support follow the resolved menu type.

Shop Menus · Menu Configuration & Inheritance

dialog:

button-width: 150
columns: 2

Copy

custom-command:
name: categories
description: Open the category menu

📍Placeholders

🔧Built-in Placeholders

The {random-last-reset_<ID>} placeholder displays the last time a random placeholder value was reset. It returns the configured placeholder.refresh.never text before the first reset.

By default, placeholder displayed in GUI will only refreshed when player open the menu or click the button. If you want to auto update placeholder value, you need enable menu.shop.update option in config.yml file. Remeber: this can even cost x20 plugin performance than normal when open the GUI. This is NOT a good choice to enable this.

If your shop ID includes _ symbol, you can replace them to - symbol to avoid plugin may not parse them correctly.

Built-in Placeholders List

Placeholder

Display Info

Where can use

{shop}

Display Shop ID (filename).

Message File
Actions

{shop-name}

Display Shop Display Name.

Shop Menu
Actions

Display Shop Menu ID.

Display Product ID.

Show whether now price is greatter or less than base price. Only use for dynamic price.

Display Purchased Items Name.

Display Menu ID.

Message File

Display Buy/Sell price.

Message File

{limit}

Display Buy/Sell limits.

Message File

Display Buy/Sell times.

Message File

Display Product Reset Refresh Time

Message File

Display Countdown Time for Product Reset Refresh Time

{last-buy-player}

Display the time interval between the last purchase of this item by a single player, in seconds. If the player has not purchased this item or the buy time has been reset, it will return 0.

PlaceholderAPI Support
amount option
PREMIUM

{last-buy-reset-player}

Display the time interval between the last buy reset or first buy time after reset (depends on the reset mode you selected, for more info, please view this page) of this item by a single player, in seconds. If the player has not purchased this item or the buy time has been reset, it will return last buy time.

PlaceholderAPI Support
amount option
PREMIUM (3.8.3+)

{last-sell-player}

Display the time interval between the last sell of this item by a single player, in seconds. If the player has not sold this item or the sell time has been reset, it will return 0.

PlaceholderAPI Support
amount option
PREMIUM

{last-sell-reset-player}

Display the time interval between the last sell reset or first sell time after reset (depends on the reset mode you selected, for more info, please view this page) of this item by a single player, in seconds. If the player has not sold this item or the sell time has been reset, it will return last sell time.

PlaceholderAPI Support
amount option
PREMIUM (3.8.3+)

{last-buy-server}

Display the time interval between the last purchase of this item by global server, in seconds. If no one has not purchased this item before or the buy time has been reset, it will return 0.

PlaceholderAPI Support
amount option
PREMIUM

If the player has not purchased this item or the buy time has been reset, it will return last buy time.

amount option

amount option

If the player has not sold this item or the sell time has been reset, it will return last sell time.

PlaceholderAPI Support

{random_<ID>}

Query random placeholder's first picked element.
For more info about random placeholder, please view Randoms Placeholders page.

Anywhere in plugin
PREMIUM

{random_<ID>;;<Number>}

Quert random placeholder specife number of picked element, if this number of picked element does not exist, we will quert the last picked element. You can set max amount of picked element by element-amount option in random placeholder.

Anywhere in plugin
PREMIUM

{random-times_<ID>}

View random placeholder refresh time.

Anywhere in plugin
PREMIUM

{random-next_<ID>}

View countdown time for random placeholder refresh time. If the refresh time is not generated, we will return a string set in config.yml.

Anywhere in plugin
PREMIUM

{conditional_<ID>}

Use conditional placeholder.
For more info, please view Conditional Placeholders page.

Anywhere in plugin
PREMIUM

{custom_<ID>}

Use custom placeholder.
For more info, please view Custom Placeholders page.

Anywhere in plugin
Free version: up to 5

{compare_
<number1>_<number2}

Compare 2 numbers. Result format can be changed in config.yml file.

Anywhere in plugin
PREMIUM

{cron_"<cronStr>"}

Print Cron format result.
Don't miss out the " symbol! Should use Quartz format.

Anywhere in plugin

{math_<mathStr>}

Calculate the math string you put. Like {math_10+50} will print 60.
Require you enable math.enabled option in config.yml file.
You can set result scale at placeholder.math.scale option.

Anywhere in plugin

Sell chest hologram placeholders

The sell chest hologram also supports {id}, {owner}, {usage}, {multiplier}, and {price}. See the Sell Chest page for the value and behavior of each placeholder.

Use built-in placeholder in amount option requires you change this option in config.yml file from

to

PlaceholderAPI Support

All built-in placeholders above that has PlaceholderAPI Support tag can be used in PlaceholderAPI expansion:

Use %ultimateshop_<shopID>_<productID>_<builtInPlaceholder>% to display built-in placeholder outsite of the plugin!

For example:

%ultimateshop_example_A_{buy-limit-player}%

Start from 2.5.6 version, you don't need put {} symbol into builtInPlaceholder arg, new format example, if you want to use this placeholderapi in our plugin, you have to use this new format becuase we will auto parse built-in placeholder into the value, this new format can avoid this:

%ultimateshop_example_A_buy-limit-player%

For random, random times, random last reset, random next, compare and conditional placeholder, you don't need specife the shop and the product, just put the placeholder after ultimateshop. For example:

%ultimateshop_{random-times_rotate}%

This don't support remove {} symbol.

New Line Symbol

Use ;; symbol if you want to start a new line, this is very useful for some people want to display price in multi lines.

📍Placeholders

⛓️Conditional Placeholder - Premium

We added {conditional} built-in placeholder in plugin.

Config

All conditional placeholder configs are stored in placeholders/conditional_placeholders folder. The file name is it's ID, for example: buy.yml means it's ID is buy. An example of it's config is like below:

Copy

mode: MAX

value:
default: 1
vip: 1.5
mvp: 2

conditions:
vip:
1:
type: permission
permission: 'group.vip'
mvp:
1:
type: permission
permission: 'group.mvp'

mode: The type of conditional placeholder.

DEFAULT: The basic mode checks whether the player meets the corresponding conditions from top to bottom, and if they do, the corresponding value will be immediately returned.

MAX: Require all values of the placeholder to be numbers, and the maximum value that meets the condition will be returned.

MIN: Similar to MAX, but min value will be returned.

conditions: The condition of each value. Each condition id section should use Condition Format.

value: The value of each condition. A option called default is the value returned that if no condition meet.

Use Placeholder

Use {conditional_<ID>} placeholder to display it's value. For more info, please view Placeholders page. For example: {conditional_buy}

Example: Conditional Product

Create a new conditional placeholder like this:

Make sure your shop includes product with ID A, B, C.

Open your shop's menu configs, find layout option:

Replace yourPlaceholderName to the file name of the conditional placeholder you used.

Previous🎲Random Placeholder - Premium

Next🗯️Custom Placeholder

Last updated 1 month ago

Config

Use Placeholder

Example: Conditional Product

Copy

mode: DEFAULT

value:
default: A # Product ID
vip: B # Product ID
mvp: C # Product ID

conditions:
vip:
1:
type: permission
permission: 'group.vip'
mvp:
1:
type: permission
permission: 'group.mvp'

Copy

dynamic-layout: true

layout:
- '000000000'
- '000`{conditional_yourPlaceholderName}``{conditional_yourPlaceholderName}``{conditional_yourPlaceholderName}`000'
- '000000000'
- 'a0003000b'

📍Placeholders

🗯️Custom Placeholder

This feature was added in version 4.5.26, and still in BETA test.

The free version can create up to 5 custom placeholders; the premium version has no limit.

The main purpose of a custom placeholder is to record desired information, such as the quantity of same of material sold, or the earning. Simply modify the placeholder value using commands in the appropriate places to achieve the desired effect.

Config

All custom placeholder configs are stored in placeholders/custom_placeholders folder. The file name is it's ID, for example: example.yml means it's ID is example. An example of it's config is like below:

Copy

type: number
default-value: "1"
per-player-value: true

type: Support value: default or number. If you only want to record numeric data, then set this to number; otherwise, set it to default.

default-value: If no value is set for this placeholder, the default value set here will be used.

per-player-value: If set to true, each player has an independent placeholder value; otherwise, all players share the same placeholder value.

Use Placeholder

Use {custom_<ID>} placeholder to display it's value. For more info, please view Placeholders page. For example: {custom_example}

Set Placeholder Value

You need to set the value of the custom placeholder by using commands.

/shop setcustomplaceholder <ID> <value> [playerName]
If per-player-result is set to true, then playerName will be a required parameter; otherwise, the playerName parameter should not be entered.

/shop addcustomplaceholder <ID> <value> [playerName]
Only work number type of custom placeholder. It will be added to the current value of the placeholder. For example, if the current value is 1 and the value parameter entered here is 5, then the final placeholder value will be replaced with 6.

Previous⛓️Conditional Placeholder - Premium

📍Placeholders

🔖Example: Discount

Create Conditional Placeholder

Use conditional placeholder with MAX or MIN mode, discount feature can be easily implemented.

Here we created 2 conditional placeholder for buy and sell.

Copy

mode: MAX

value:
default: 1
vip: 1.5
mvp: 2

conditions:
vip:
1:
type: permission
permission: 'group.vip'
mvp:
1:
type: permission
permission: 'group.mvp'

Use the placeholder in product configs

5 is base price, then add {conditional_buy} or {conditional_sell} before it.

If player meet vip condition, the {condition_buy} will return 0.5, so he will only need pay 0.5*4 which means only 2.5 coins!

Auto Apply Discount

Start from 2.3.2, you can auto apply conditional placeholder to all prices! Just configure it in config.yml file. This can helps you auto add discount for each products.

It is recommend you disable this feature for dynamic price, if you want to enable it for dynamic price too, just change black-dynamic-price option to false here!

If you want to disable this feature for specified shops, use black-shops option.

Previous🗯️Custom Placeholder

Next🔄Dynamic Price

Use the placeholder in product configs

Auto Apply Discount

Copy

mode: MIN

value:
default: 1
vip: 0.5
mvp: 0.3

conditions:
vip:
1:
type: permission
permission: 'group.vip'
mvp:
1:
type: permission
permission: 'group.mvp'

Copy

items:
A:
price-mode: CLASSIC_ALL
product-mode: CLASSIC_ALL
products:
1:
material: sea_lantern
lore:
- '%player_health%'
buy-prices:
1:
economy-plugin: Vault
amount: '{conditional_buy} * 5' # <--- Changed line
start-apply: 0
placeholder: '{amount} Coins'
sell-prices:
1:
economy-plugin: Vault
amount: '{conditional_sell} * 5' # <--- Changed line
start-apply: 0
placeholder: '{amount} Coins'

Copy

placeholder:
auto-settings:
add-conditional-in-all-price-amount:
enabled: false
buy-placeholder: buy
sell-placeholder: sell
black-dynamic-price: true
black-shops:
- 'example'

📍Placeholders

🎲Random Placeholder - Premium

We added {random} built-in placeholder in premium version. You can use random placeholder at the options that support the use of placeholders to randomly select the value. For example, random price amount, random reset time, random stock, etc.

Config

All random placeholder configs are stored in placeholders/random_placeholders folder. The file name is it's ID, for example: rotate.yml means it's ID is rotate. An example of it's config is like below:

Copy

reset-mode: TIMED
reset-time: '00:00:00'
per-player-element: true
element-sort: true
element-amount: 5
elements:
A:
rate: 1
conditions:
1:
type: permission
permission: 'test.permission'
B:
rate: 5
C:
rate: 2
D:
rate: 5
E:
rate: 2
F:
rate: 15
G:
rate: 7

per-player-element: If set to false, all players will use the same value output by random placeholder. For example, player 1 will get A, and player 2 will get the same value, even if the player reaches 100 million, the value will be the same. If set to true, the values output by each player will be different from each other, and conditions can be set for elements. If an element does not meet the conditions, the player will never be able to extract it.

This option is NOT recommended to be changed after enabling this random placeholder, as it may cause the plugin to detect errors (such as a per player random placeholder appearing in the global database) and output a prompt in the console that cannot be blocked.
The random placeholder of per player cannot be applied to global server scenarios, and vice versa. Random placeholders without per player enabled cannot be applied to per player scenarios. For example, when using the resetrandomplaceholder command, the per player's random placeholder must enter the player name in the command parameters, while the per player's random placeholder cannot enter the player name in the command parameters, otherwise the plugin will prompt an error.

element-sort: If set to false, the result of the random placeholder will be out of order. For example, if you set elements to A, B, C, D, E and the randomly generated result is B, D, the result of the random placeholder may be B, D, or D, B. However, if set to true, the result will be output strictly in the order of elements, and the output result can only be B, D. (Added in 3.12.2)

reset-mode/reset-time: Please view below to know.

element-amount: The amount of the element will picked in this placeholder. (Added in 3.1.0)

elements: The random element what placeholder will picked.

There are two ways to express elements.

The first is to write all elements in the form of a list, where the rate of each element is equal and without any conditions. Support use ~ symbol means pick random number, for example, 5~100 means pick one random number from 5 to 100.

The second is to use the form of subsections, where each element has options for rate and conditions to fill in. The conditions option is not required and only support when per-player-element option being set to true. (Added in 3.12.0)

Use Placeholder

Use {random_<ID>;;<Number>} placeholder to display it's value, like {random_daily;;2} will query daily random placeholder's second element picked. For more info, please view Placeholders page. For example of this placeholder usage, please view Daily Shop page.

Use {random-times_<ID>} placeholder to display the reset time of the placeholder, like {random-times_daily}.

Use {random-last-reset_<ID>} placeholder to display the last time its value was reset, like {random-last-reset_daily}. It displays Never until the first reset occurs.

Reset Placeholder

You can reset placeholder by setting reset-mode and reset-time option or use /shop resetrandomplaceholder command.

Reset Mode

Supports below reset mode:

NEVER

ONCE: Each time the placeholer is used, it will reset and is not able to use in the price, as the price seen by the player opening the shop and the actual transaction result are calculated twice, so you cannot achieve price synchronization which means price player seen in shop will not same as the price player actually cost.

TIMER: It will reset after the time you specify, for example, after 5 hours.

TIMED: It will be reset at the corresponding time, such as 8:15 pm.

CUSTOM: Directly enter the reset time in reset time, and the plugin will not perform any calculations. Recommend obtain reset time through the Placeholder API results. You need set time format at time-format option to helps us know how does your PlaceholderAPI results be like.

We first generate the reset time after the random placeholder is used. CUSTOM targets are re-evaluated when the placeholder or its refresh time is queried, and the pooled task is updated when the target changes.

Do NOT use COOLDOWN_TIMER, COOLDOWN_TIMED, or COOLDOWN_CUSTOM reset mode here; they are not supported by random placeholders.

Reset Time

Different reset modes require different values to be filled in here. Supports placeholders, the placeholder used here must be on the server side, which means that all players receive the same value.

NEVER

Don't need anything here.

TIMER

You can enter 3 to 5 numbers here, separated by a : symbol between each number. For example: 15:00:00.

Each number from right to left represents:

Seconds

Minutes

Hours

Days

Months

In this example, represents 15 hours later. Which means: if now time is 2023-09-04 12:00:00. Will reset after 15 hours, which means 2023-09-05 03:00:00.

TIMED

The composition of TIMED and TIMER is almost identical, but the first three digits from the right-hand side represent the time of day. Let's also take 15:00:00 as an example:

If now time is 2023-09-04 12:00:00, will reset at 2023-09-04 15:00:00.

This is the result obtained with days set to 0. If you set it to 1, we will add another day, and that's it.

If you want to do a daily shop, days should be set to 0, and if you want to do a weekly shop, days should be set to 6. Because you need to reset the number of times on the last day, not on the second day after the last day, right?

This type of reset mode also supports set multi reset time, each reset time use ;; to splite, we will pick up the earliest reset time. For example:

In this example, this random placeholder will reset at 19:00 and 20:00 every day.

CUSTOM

You only need to enter a Placeholder API placeholder here, and the result of the placeholder must include the complete year, month, day, hour, minute, and second. You also need to enter its format in the time-format option. The same CUSTOM target executes only once; lastResetTime is persisted so it will not repeat after a restart.

Cron Reset (Weekly Reset/Monthly Reset)

You can use Cron format in reset time.

Set reset mode to CUSTOM.

Use {cron_"<Cron Expression>"} built-in placeholder in reset time. Don't miss out the " symbol. The <Cron Expression> should use Quartz format.

For example:

You can obtain the Cron expression you want by asking ChatGPT or use this tool. For example, the Cron expression in this example means to reset at 0:00 every Thursday. We do not provide any help related to how to write Cron expression.

You MUST make sure that time format of the result of Cron placeholder (set it in config.yml file) and the time-format you set here is same. By default, they are same.

Testing

You can know about the reset time of the random placeholder by using the command /shop getplaceholdvalue {random times_<placeholdID>}. And by using /shop getplaceplaceervalue {random_<placeholdID>} to know the current value of the random placeholder, you can compare whether the value before reset is the same after reset by this way.

Example: Random Price

Create new random placeholder

In this example, we create a new random placeholder config called price.yml at random_placeholder folder.

Set dynamic value in your product configs

Use {random_price} placeholder at any product's price config. Like I add this placeholder in this product:

Reset Placeholder

Reset Mode

Reset Time

TIMER

Example: Random Price

Create new random placeholder

Set dynamic value in your product configs

Copy

elements:
- 'A'
- 'B'
- 'C'

Copy

elements:
# Random number from 5 to 100.
- '5~100'

Copy

per-player-element: true # <--- Only support use condition when this option to true
elements:
A:
rate: 1
conditions:
1:
type: permission
permission: 'test.permission'
B:
rate: 5
C:
rate: 2

Copy

reset-mode: 'TIMED'
reset-time: '20:00:00;;19:00:00'

Copy

reset-mode: 'CUSTOM'
reset-time: '{cron_"0 0 0 ? * 5}'
time-format: 'yyyy-MM-dd HH:mm:ss'

Copy

reset-mode: TIMED
reset-time: '00:00:00'
element-sort: true
element-amount: 50
elements:
# Random number from 5 to 100.
- '5~100'
- '4~40'
- '53~530'
- '32~140'
- '55~140'

Copy

items:
A:
price-mode: CLASSIC_ALL
product-mode: CLASSIC_ALL
products:
1:
material: coal
amount: 1
buy-prices:
1:
economy-plugin: Vault
amount: '{random_price;;1}' # <--- Changed line
placeholder: '&6{amount} Coins'
start-apply: 0

🛍️Shops

⚡Example: Common Usage

Copy

settings:
menu: 'example-shop-menu'
buy-more: true
shop-name: 'Blocks Shop'
hide-message: false

items:
A:
# ...
B:
# ...
C:
# ...

Product A - VIP Limits

This example has sell limits, which means player can only sell this product X times every day.

Sell limits can help your server has "economy balance", even player has 10000 rotten flesh, he still can only sell 64x in this example.

In this example, VIP players will have 50% more sell limits, which can help your server has more supporter.

In this example, sell limits will reset at local time '0:00:00', which means midnight.

In this example, we set price and product mode has CLASSIC_ prefix, this will save server performance, you should use this when you didn't set start-apply option to other value in prices section.

Product B - Experience Price

Product A only brought an item from vanilla, let's try to obtain an item from a third-party plugin! Meanwhile, using Vault as an economy is too boring! Let's try something different, such as vanilla's experience points!

To get how to write hook-plugin option and hook-item option, please read Item Format page.

To get how to write economy-type option or economy-plugin option, please read Economy Format page.

Product C - Command Product

Sometimes, we do not support the third-party plugin items you are using, so you can solve this by creating a command shop!

You can first enter the command /shop generateitemformat to generate an Item Format and fill it into the display-item option. This way, even if you haven't set any items, the plugin can still display the item in the store by reading the item in the display-item option.

This product didn't set sell-prices option, so it can not be sold.

After purchase, we will execute buy-actions which includes execute the command you set. For more info about Actions, please read Action Format page.

Seasonal Product

Previous💹Price Modifier

Next🌱Example: Stock (like in life)

Last updated 4 months ago

Product A - VIP Limits

Product B - Experience Price

Product C - Command Product

Seasonal Product

Copy

items:
A:
price-mode: CLASSIC_ALL
product-mode: CLASSIC_ALL
products:
1:
material: rotten_flesh
amount: 1
buy-prices:
1:
economy-plugin: Vault
amount: 10
placeholder: '&6{amount} Coins'
start-apply: 0
sell-prices:
1:
economy-plugin: Vault
amount: 0.8
placeholder: '&6{amount} Coins'
start-apply: 0
sell-limits:
global: 1280
default: 64
vip: 192
sell-limits-conditions:
vip:
1:
type: permission
permission: 'group.vip'
sell-limits-reset-mode: 'TIMED'
sell-limits-reset-time: '00:00:00'

Copy

items:
A:
# ...
B:
products:
1:
hook-plugin: MMOItems # Plugin Name
hook-item: AXE;;EXECUTIONER_AXE # Item ID
amount: 1
price-mode: ANY
product-mode: ALL
buy-prices:
1:
economy-type: exp
amount: 1
start-apply: 0
placeholder: '1 Exp'
sell-prices:
1:
economy-type: exp
amount: 1
start-apply: 0
placeholder: '1 Exp'

Copy

items:
A:
# ...
B:
# ...
C:
price-mode: CLASSIC_ALL
product-mode: CLASSIC_ALL
display-item:
name: 'Chicken Spawner'
material: PLAYER_HEAD

🛍️Shops

🏆Example: Daily Rewards

Rewards is very similar to Shops, but rewards don't have any price, and usually, it needs to be coordinated with the menu system.

Example for Daily Rewards

Create new shop config at /shops/ folder, then create new product like this:

Copy

A:
price-mode: CLASSIC_ALL
product-mode: CLASSIC_ALL
products:
1:
material: DIAMOND
amount: 16
2:
material: IRON_INGOT
amount: 64
3:
economy-plugin: Vault
amount: 1500
buy-prices:
1:
economy-type: exp
amount: 0
placeholder: 'Free'
buy-limits:
default: '1'
buy-times-reset-mode: 'TIMED'
buy-times-reset-time: '00:00:00'

In this example, players can purchase this "product" once each day, and because the price is free, so we can consider it as "reward".

If you want to make VIP players have bonus 64x bread, then just use our conditions system, like this:

If you also want to VIP players have 50% chance to get bonus 1x diamond sword, then we need create a new random placeholder at config.yml:

Then also use condition system:

🛍️Shops

📅Example: Daily Shop/Rotating Shop

This example will only work for PREMIUM version of UltimateShop!
This example is using random placeholder to display different product in one shop, you can also use conditinal placeholder to make different condition display different product, for example of it, please view here.

Create random placeholder

We need to create a random placeholder.

Save the random placeholder as placeholders/random_placeholders/daily.yml.

The placeholder draws five product IDs from elements each day. The menu uses the first three results. Each ID in elements must match a key under items in the shop file.

Please view Random Placeholder page for more info about random placeholder.

Copy

reset-mode: TIMED
reset-time: '00:00:00'
element-amount: 5
per-player-element: false
element-sort: true
elements:
- 'redstone'
- 'iron_ingot'
- 'gold_ingot'
- 'copper_ingot'
- 'diamond'
- 'lapis_lazuli'
- 'emerald'

Configure Shop

The various options used in this example are detailed on the Shops page. If you are unsure of the purpose of each option, please refer to that article.

Add products A through G; their IDs must match the placeholder elements.

Configure Menu

The various options used in this example are detailed on the Menus page. If you are unsure of the purpose of each option, please refer to that article.

Each product slot tries the random placeholder first. If that product cannot appear in the slot, P shows the product in the corresponding position in the shop file.

Showcase

Next🏆Example: Daily Rewards

🛍️Shops

🌱Example: Stock (like in life)

This means real life stock, for set limit of buy/sell times, please view Products page.

UltimateShop does not store a separate warehouse inventory. Instead, use server-wide transaction counts: the number sold becomes the buy limit.

Copy

Player A sells 5 items
↓
server sell count becomes 5
↓
shop buy limit becomes 5
↓
Player B can buy at most those 5 items

Set dynamic value for your product configs

The plugin itself does not store stock data, however, the logic of stock is essentially that player A sells an item to the shop, then the shop can sell the item sold by player A to player B. You only need to set a buy limit, and the value of the buy limit is the number of times the product is sold globally, that is, the placeholder: {server-times-server}. Read dynamic prices before read this page. Similar to Dynamic Prices, if you want to make stock system, do it in buy-limits option and put {server-times-server} placeholder in it, for example:

Copy

A:
price-mode: ALL
product-mode: CLASSIC_ALL
products:
1:
material: echo_shard
amount: 1
buy-prices:
1:
economy-plugin: EcoBits
economy-type: quest_points
amount: 5
placeholder: '&b{amount} Quest Points'
start-apply: 0
sell-prices:
1:
economy-plugin: EcoBits
economy-type: quest_points
amount: 5
placeholder: '&b{amount} Quest Points'
start-apply: 0
buy-limits:
global: '{sell-times-server}'
buy-times-reset-mode: 'NEVER'
buy-times-reset-time: '00:00:00'

We changed:

price-mode option to ANY or ALL.

buy-limits option to {sell-times-server} . For sell limits, you need write {buy-times-server} here. Replace the placeholder to {buy-times-player} and {sell-times-player} to make the stock be per player.

buy-limits-reset-mode option to 'NEVER'

In this way, we can ensure that players can only purchase items in the same quantity as the sell times (means restocking by other players sell it)

Variations

Replace server placeholders with player placeholders for a personal stock system.

Use {buy-times-server} in a sell limit to model demand before supply.

Add a fixed amount in a math expression to provide initial stock.

Combine this with dynamic prices so scarce products become more expensive.

Restock Manually

Restocking means changing transaction counts or the formula that determines the limit.

This content of auto reset buy/sell times is introduced on this page.

Use command /shop setselltimes <shop> <product> global <newStockPlusNowSellTimes>. If you want to restock 50 amount, and the now sell time is 75, then you need put 125 to <newStockPlusNowSellTimes>.

Next📅Example: Daily Shop/Rotating Shop

🛍️Shops

💹Price Modifier

This feature is offered to users as an early experimental feature and will be officially available after version 5.0.0.

The feature is still under testing and may change before its final release.

Price Modifier allows UltimateShop to adjust a product's sell reward according to properties of the actual item being sold.

For example, you can:

Reduce the sell price of a damaged item.

Read a numeric value from item lore and use it as the sell price.

Read a numeric value from NBT and use it as the sell price.

Apply different multipliers according to MythicChanger match-item rules.

Price modifiers are configured globally under:

Copy

sell:
price-modifier:

However, a product will only use them when the following option is enabled in that product:

Copy

price-modifier: true

See Products for more information about product options.

Item Matching vs Price Modifier

Item matching and Price Modifier are two different systems.

Item matching decides:

Can this item be sold as this product?

Price Modifier decides:

How much should this actual item be worth?

The simplified selling process is:

This distinction is especially important when a Price Modifier uses item data that is expected to change between different items.

For example, suppose a shop sells and buys a diamond sword.

The configured product contains:

But the player tries to sell:

If the two items fail the sell matching check, the damaged sword will never reach the durability Price Modifier.

The Price Modifier does not automatically make differently damaged, named, enchanted, or otherwise modified items match the same product.

Bukkit and ItemFormat sell methods

UltimateShop supports two global item matching methods:

and:

With Bukkit, UltimateShop uses Bukkit's item similarity check. Differences in item data such as durability, lore, enchantments, names, components, or NBT may cause an item to no longer match the configured product.

ItemFormat provides more control because specific item properties can be ignored during the matching process.

Example:

In this example, different durability values do not prevent items from matching the product.

Ignoring a property in item-format.ignore-key only affects item matching.

It does not remove that information from the actual item.

For example, if damage is ignored during matching, the durability Price Modifier can still read the actual damage value of the item being sold.

For more information about item matching, see Custom Item Match Method.

You can also hold an item and use:

to inspect its ItemFormat data and determine the correct key path.

Basic Configuration

A complete example can look like this:

Modifiers are processed in configuration order.

Enable Price Modifier for a Product

Price Modifier is disabled for products by default.

Enable it by adding:

to the product configuration.

Example:

The configured sell price is still the product's base sell price.

Price Modifier changes that price according to the actual item being sold.

Durability Modifier

The durability modifier reduces the sell price according to how much durability the item has lost.

Example:

Non-damageable items use a multiplier of 1.

minimum-multiplier

You can prevent damaged items from becoming almost worthless:

For example, even if the durability calculation produces:

the final modifier will be:

minimum-price

You can also specify a minimum numeric price:

This prevents the calculated numeric price from dropping below the configured value.

minimum-price and minimum-multiplier can be used separately or together.

Item matching for durability

If damaged and undamaged items should be treated as the same sellable product, their different damage values must not prevent item matching.

The recommended configuration is:

This creates the following behavior:

damage is ignored only when deciding whether the item matches the product. The modifier still receives the actual damaged ItemStack.

Lore Value Modifier

The Lore modifier reads a numeric value from an item's lore.

Example item:

Configuration:

The first matching lore line supplies the numeric value.

SET

With:

the captured value becomes the price of each sold trade unit.

For example:

When multiple trade units are sold together, UltimateShop keeps the result consistent with selling those units separately.

With:

the captured value multiplies the current price.

For example:

Numeric safety options

The following options prevent invalid or unreasonable values from being used:

Values outside the configured range are ignored.

Values longer than maximum-number-length are also ignored.

Missing, invalid, or unmatched values leave the current price unchanged.

Unsupported operations are reported as configuration errors and the affected modifier is ignored.

Item matching for Lore

If the lore used by the modifier is expected to differ between individual items, the lore difference must not prevent the item from matching the product.

For example:

If both should be sellable as the same product, you can use:

Ignoring lore during item matching does not prevent the Lore Price Modifier from reading the actual lore of the sold item.

However, ignoring the complete lore property means all lore differences are ignored during sell matching.

Only do this if all such lore differences are acceptable for that product.

For more precise item matching, consider using product-level match-item rules.

NBT Value Modifier

The NBT modifier reads a numeric value from the item's NBT data.

This modifier requires the NBTAPI plugin.

Example:

Nested NBT paths are supported:

value-type

Supported values are:

AUTO automatically attempts to obtain a usable numeric value.

Numeric strings are also accepted.

SET and MULTIPLY

The NBT modifier supports the same operations as the Lore modifier.

SET

uses the NBT value as the unit price.

MULTIPLY

multiplies the current price by the NBT value.

Invalid values

The modifier leaves the current price unchanged when the NBT value is:

Missing.

Invalid.

Non-numeric.

Non-finite.

Outside minimum-value and maximum-value.

Longer than maximum-number-length.

Item matching for NBT

If the NBT value used as the dynamic price differs between individual items, that value may also need to be ignored during ItemFormat matching.

For example, after inspecting the item with:

you may find a key such as:

You can then configure:

Whenever possible, ignore only the specific dynamic NBT key.

Avoid ignoring the entire nbt section unless you intentionally want all NBT differences to be ignored.

For example:

may cause many otherwise different items to be treated as the same product.

Use /shop generateitemformat to determine the most specific ItemFormat key available for your item.

The match_item modifier uses MythicChanger match-item rules.

This modifier requires MythicChanger.

If MythicChanger is unavailable, the modifier has no effect.

Invalid values or unsupported operations are reported through UltimateShop's error manager and the affected rule is ignored.

Multiple Match Item Rules

The mode option controls what happens when multiple rules match.

Supported modes are:

MAX / HIGHEST

Uses the highest resulting multiplier.

For example:

result:

MIN / LOWEST

Processes matching rules in configuration order.

ADD values add to the accumulated multiplier.

Multiple Price Modifiers

All enabled Price Modifiers under:

are applied in configuration order.

For example:

the durability modifier is processed before the lore modifier.

The result of an earlier modifier becomes part of the current price used by later modifiers.

Therefore, changing the configuration order may also change the final result.

Selling Flow

Products with:

require access to the actual ItemStack being sold.

Because of this, sell flows that cannot provide an actual item stack do not directly display or execute the normal sell price.

When the player chooses:

or:

Buy actions, amount-selection actions, and custom actions are not redirected by this behavior.

Bedrock forms and dialog shop lists open the product information view first, where buy and sell remain separate actions.

A product with Price Modifier enabled can still be sold through the item sell menu even if:

is configured for that product.

Precision and Rounding

Applying a multiplier is treated as a mathematical calculation.

UltimateShop rounds calculation results using:

to the decimal places configured by:

This applies to:

Price Modifiers.

Global sell multipliers.

Price previews.

Final sell rewards.

For more information, see Math Calculate Format.

Transaction Event Behavior

During an actual transaction, the sell price stored in the post-event GiveResult is authoritative.

If another plugin changes the sell price through:

UltimateShop preserves that changed result.

When different actual ItemStacks need different Price Modifier results, recalculated stack prices are used only to determine how the already-authoritative total should be distributed between those stacks before their item-aware modifiers are applied.

This prevents a transaction event's price modification from being accidentally replaced by a second normal price calculation.

Common Problems

Damaged items cannot be sold

If you are using:

but damaged items cannot be sold, check your global sell method.

Using:

may cause different damage values to fail item matching.

Consider:

with:

Lore modifier does not run

If the item's lore differs from the product item, the item may be rejected before the modifier is reached.

If that lore difference is intentional, configure the item matching system accordingly.

NBT modifier does not run

Check both systems separately:

Can the actual item match the product?

Can the NBT modifier find the configured NBT key?

Use:

to inspect ItemFormat matching data.

The NBT key used by the Price Modifier and the ItemFormat key used by ignore-key represent related item data, but they are configured for different purposes.

Price Modifier API

Global item-aware Price Modifiers are managed by PriceModifierManager in cn.superiormc.ultimateshop.managers. It loads sell.price-modifier through the existing ConfigManager and rebuilds the configured chain on plugin reload. Custom factories remain registered across /shop reload.

A custom implementation only needs to implement PriceModifier and register a factory:

Register after UltimateShop has enabled, using a unique type ID. As with the action manager, registering an existing ID leaves the existing type unchanged. Registering a new type rebuilds the configured chain, so configured entries using that type become available immediately.

Each modifier receives:

The player.

The actual sold ItemStack.

The current numeric price.

It returns a non-negative multiplier.

The four-argument overload also receives the number of trade units for modifiers that require quantity-aware behavior, such as SET.

Modifiers configured under:

are applied in configuration order.

Registering a new factory, or replacing an existing factory with the same type, immediately rebuilds the configured Price Modifier chain.

A server reload is not required.
